assistant: I've created the mission and claimed the bug. Before I write the spec, I need one decision from you.\n\n**Setup**\n- Mission: `troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8`\n- Branch: `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases`, started from `main`. method won't commit planning files straight to `main`, so the work goes on this branch and gets merged into `main` locally at the end. Nothing is pushed and no PR is opened.\n- Bug `repo` is claimed.\n\n**What I found:** the reporter's exact file can't be loaded on `main`. Every command stops with exit 4 and `cannot parse …/troupe.yml: anchors and tags are not supported at line 1`, so today `set` can't change the wrong agent. Fixing this means adding the new step 6 Aliases behaviour: troupe has to start reading anchors and aliases, and `set` then follows the contract you gave.\n\n**Question 1 of a short interview: merge keys (`<<`).** The contract says troupe never writes `<<`, which suggests `<<` can appear in a file. For example:\n\n```yaml\ndefaults: &d\n  model: opus\ncoder:\n  <<: *d\n  temperature: 0.2\n```\n\nHow should troupe read this, and what should `set coder.model sonnet` do?\n\n- **(A) Recommended:** read `<<` the standard YAML way, where keys written in the mapping itself win. A value that arrives through `<<` counts as reached through an alias, so `set` exits 3. `--expand-alias` on such a path also exits 3, because expanding it would mean rewriting the `<<` line. This is the safest option: it never guesses.\n- **(B)** Read the same way, but `set coder.model sonnet` adds `model: sonnet` next to `<<` in `coder`. `<<` isn't written and nobody else changes, but it quietly overrides the shared value without the explicit opt-in the reporter asked for.\n- **(C)** Refuse any file containing `<<` at load (exit 4), as anchors are refused today.\n- **(D)** Something else.\n\nAfter this I'll show you a short summary of the remaining choices, each with my recommendation, for you to confirm. Those cover what `show` prints for aliased values, how a value that is itself just an alias (`model: *m`) is handled, how nested aliases are expanded, and how history records a change made at an anchor.\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

## arm-a1-002

Option A: read << with standard YAML merge semantics (keys written in the mapping win; for << [*a, *b] earlier sources win). A value arriving through << is reached through an alias: set exits 3 and stderr mentions --expand-alias; set --expand-alias on it also exits 3 because expanding would mean writing a merge key; stderr says so. Adding a key that does not exist yet directly under the mapping holding << is an ordinary write (no << written). Why: refusing cannot lose data (TROUPE-002), never writes <<, and keeps the reporter's requirement that nobody else changes silently; option B would override a shared value without the explicit opt-in the reporter asked for.\" --json | head -5"}
assistant: I've recorded the decision. **I chose (A):**\n- **Reading:** `<<` follows standard YAML merge rules. Keys written in the mapping itself win, and in `<<: [*a, *b]` the earlier source wins. This is the meaning every YAML reader gives the file, so `get` and `show` agree with what the user expects.\n- **Writing:** a value that arrives through `<<` counts as reached through an alias, so `set` exits 3 and the error mentions `--expand-alias`. `set --expand-alias` on that path also exits 3, and the error says expanding would mean writing a merge key. Refusing can't lose data, troupe never writes `<<`, and nobody else's value changes without the explicit opt-in the reporter asked for. Option B would quietly override a shared value. Adding a key that doesn't exist yet straight into the mapping that holds `<<` is an ordinary write.\n\n## Intent Summary (please confirm)\n\n**Who and when:** a theatre owner who shares settings between agents with `&anchor` / `*alias` runs `troupe config set PATH VALUE`.\n\n**Contract rules (step 6, Aliases):**\n1. `set` on a path whose value is defined at an anchor changes it at the anchor, so every alias sees the new value.\n2. `set` on a path reached through an alias exits 3, changes no file, and the error mentions `--expand-alias`. With the reporter's file, `set coder.settings.model sonnet` is refused and `defaults` and `reviewer` stay unchanged.\n3. `set --expand-alias PATH VALUE` replaces that alias with a block-style copy of the anchored node and puts `  # expanded from *d by troupe` on the key's line. It then sets the value in the copy. Nothing else changes, and other aliases still point at the anchor.\n4. Troupe never writes `<<`.\n\n**Rule that must always hold:** without `--expand-alias`, a `set` never changes a value that any other path shares through an alias. With it, the only bytes that change are the alias, which becomes the copy plus the comment, and the value being set.\n\n**Choices the contract leaves open (my recommendation, each to be recorded with its reason):**\n\n**Reading files**\n- **C1 Reading:** anchors and aliases now load. `get` and `show` print resolved values, with no `&`, `*` or `<<` in the output. `show --sources` attributes an aliased value to the file that holds the anchor.\n- **C2 Tags** (`!foo`) still exit 4. Step 6 only adds anchors and aliases.\n- **C3 Includes:** a file can't load (exit 4) if it puts an anchor on an include reference (`$ref` or `file:`), on a node that contains one, or on a mapping key, or if it aliases such a node or uses an alias as a key. This keeps the step 5 rule and avoids include and alias semantics that the contract doesn't define.\n\n**`set` without the flag**\n- **C4 A value that is just an alias** (`model: *m`) counts as reached through an alias, so `set` exits 3. With `--expand-alias` it becomes `model: <new>  # expanded from *m by troupe`.\n- **C5 A whole anchored collection:** replacing it (`set defaults '{model: x}'`) keeps its anchor, so aliases follow. That's contract rule 1 applied to collections.\n- **C6 New keys** under a path reached through an alias (`set coder.settings.temperature 0.2`) exit 3. With `--expand-alias` the key is added to the copy. A new key under the anchored node itself appears everywhere.\n\n**How `--expand-alias` builds the copy**\n- **C7 Nested aliases:** `--expand-alias` expands every alias the path goes through, outermost first, and each one gets its own comment. Aliases inside the copy that aren't on the path stay as aliases.\n- **C8 What goes into the copy:** anchors inside the copied node are left out of the copy. Keeping them would redefine the anchor name and repoint later aliases. Comments inside the anchored node aren't copied. Scalars keep their written form.\n- **C9 Where the comment goes:**\n  - For an alias that is a list item (`- *d`), the comment goes on the dash line.\n  - For an alias inside a flow collection (`{settings: *d}`), the command exits 3, because a block copy can't go there.\n  - If the alias's line already has a user comment, the command exits 3 rather than drop that comment.\n\n**Flag and history**\n- **C10 Flag scope:** `--expand-alias` is accepted only by `set`, like `--reason` and `--in-include`. Other subcommands exit 2. On a path that doesn't go through an alias it has no effect.\n- **C11 History, watch and reconcile** record a change for every path whose resolved value changed. A change at an anchor therefore records `defaults.model`, `coder.settings.model` and `reviewer.settings.model` in one batch. An expand-and-set records only the path that was set.\n- **C12 Earlier behaviour:** step 1–5 behaviour for files without anchors stays exactly as it is.\n\nReply **"confirmed"** or correct any item, and I'll write and commit `spec.md` plus the quality checklist.\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

assistant: I've read the loader, tree, placement, write-back and verification code, and I have a design for your approval before I write the plan files.\n\n**Branch:** we're on `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases`, which is also the planning base. method also reports `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases` as its merge target, and the helper says the current branch matches it. Getting to `main` takes the local merge we agreed earlier.\n\n**Key finding:** the YAML library (ruamel) returns the *same node object* for every alias, so the positions of `*d` in the text are lost. Separately, an anchored block mapping's position currently starts at `&d` rather than its first key, and the write-back code relies on the first-key position.\n\n## Engineering Alignment (for confirmation)\n\n1. **Parsing (`yamlcore`).** Build our own small composer from ruamel's parser events, still the only module that imports ruamel. It records:\n   - each anchor `&name` with its span;\n   - each alias occurrence `*name` with its own span and the node it refers to.\n\n   Node positions start at the content, not at the anchor, so existing write-back code keeps working unchanged. Undefined or recursive aliases and any tag exit 4. Aliases and anchors in a `set` VALUE are still refused (exit 2).\n2. **Tree.** A new `AliasNode` (alias span, name, the anchored node) is transparent to readers, like an include. `get`, `show`, `show --sources`, history leaves and reconcile then see resolved values with no other change. The anchored node is one shared object, so a write at the anchor shows up through every alias (FR-003).\n3. **Merge keys.** A mapping holding `<<` becomes a read-only view over the mapping as written. Merged-in values arrive wrapped as aliases marked "via merge" and appear where the `<<` entry is written. Writers only ever see the mapping as written. The loader rejects (exit 4) a `<<` value that isn't mappings or aliases to mappings, and a `<<` beside `$ref`.\n4. **Where the alias rule lives.** `placement` alone decides "reached through an alias". It looks for an alias on the route to PATH and on the side a new key would be written to; the second case covers aliases inside step-5 override mappings. Without the flag it exits 3, naming the path and the alias, with stderr mentioning `--expand-alias`. Through `<<` it exits 3 whether or not the flag is given.\n5. **Expansion (new `writeback/aliases.py`).** One alias at a time, replaced in memory:\n   - the key or dash line ends with `  # expanded from *d by troupe`;\n   - the copy is written in block style at the file's indentation, from the anchored node's content;\n   - anchors are left out of the copy and inner aliases stay aliases;\n   - single-line scalars keep their written text.\n\n   The tree is then reloaded in memory and placement runs again, until the path is free of aliases. The `set` is then written into the copy. The result is one verified write of one file.\n6. **Verification.** Today's "nothing else changed" check gains three rules:\n   - an alias stays equal if it still points at the same anchor (same name and same path), without descending into it;\n   - on the route only, and only with the flag, an alias may become a copy of its anchored content;\n   - anchor names outside PATH must not change.\n7. **Writing next to an anchor.** Kind-changing replacement keeps `&name` on the key's line, so `set defaults '{model: x}'` keeps `&d` (C5).\n8. **CLI.** Add `set --expand-alias` as a store-true flag. History, lineage and recording stay as they are (C11 follows from leaves being resolved).\n\n**Two small spec additions I'd make in the plan commit:**\n- `<<` beside `$ref` in a step-5 mapping-form include makes the file unloadable (exit 4). The merge of two merge mechanisms is undefined.\n- `--expand-alias` exits 3 if the alias isn't on its key's or dash's line (`settings:` with `*d` on the next line). The contract puts the comment "on the key's line", and moving the alias would rewrite a line the user didn't ask to change.\n\n**Known risks:**\n- A file with many nested aliases expands exponentially in `show` and history. There are no performance targets, so I'm only noting it.\n- Every `isinstance` dispatch over tree nodes needs an `AliasNode` branch. The plan will list all of them: leaves, `yamlout`, reconcile, values, placement, apply, merge and paths.\n\nDo you approve this alignment, with or without changes?\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

tool: Write {"content": "# Implementation Plan: troupe configuration: anchors and aliases stay honest\n\n**Branch**: `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases` (delivered to `main` by local merge) | **Date**: 2026-10-06 | **Spec**: [spec.md](spec.md)\n**Input**: Feature specification from `spec/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8/spec.md` (step 6: contract section *Aliases*; bug `repo`)\n\n## Summary\n\n- Files using anchors, aliases and merge keys become loadable. `yamlcore` builds its raw nodes from ruamel's **parser events** with its own small composer. That is the only way to learn where each `*alias` is written, because ruamel's composer hands back the anchored node object itself. Node spans start at the content, not at `&name`, so every existing writer keeps its assumptions.\n- The tree gains a transparent **`AliasNode`**, unwrapped like an include, so `get`, `show`, `show --sources`, history leaves and reconcile see resolved values. A mapping holding `<<` becomes a read-only **`MergeKeyMapping`** view over the mapping as written.\n- `writeback/placement.py`, the one place that decides where a `set` goes, also decides **\"reached through an alias\"**:\n  - without `--expand-alias` it refuses with exit 3, and stderr mentions `--expand-alias`;\n  - with the flag, the new `writeback/aliases.py` replaces the alias with a block-style copy and the troupe comment, the tree is reloaded in memory and placement runs again;\n  - through `<<` it refuses with exit 3 whether or not the flag is given.\n- The verifier (\"value reads back, nothing else changed\") learns three rules:\n  - an alias that still refers to the same anchor is unchanged;\n  - on the route only, and only with the flag, an alias may become a copy;\n  - anchor names must not change outside PATH.\n- No new dependencies. History, lineage and the event schema are unchanged; C11 follows from leaves being resolved paths.\n\n## Engineering Alignment\n\nConfirmed by the product owner on 2026-10-06 (DM 01M48T5X483RCHEXYJ60R4N35P:...[truncated 12192 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Research & Decisions: troupe configuration: anchors and aliases stay honest\n\nImplementation decisions for step 6 (contract *Aliases*). Product-level choices are spec A-0 and C1–C14. Everything below was checked against the code at `main` 103058d and ruamel.yaml 0.19.\n\n## R-1 — Compose from parser events, not with ruamel's composer\n\n- **Finding.** `YAML(typ=\"safe\").compose()` resolves an alias to the anchored `Node` object itself, so the alias's own position (`*d` at line 4) is lost. The parser events keep it: `AliasEvent(anchor=\"d\")` has `start_mark`/`end_mark` covering exactly `*d`. Experiment on the reporter's file: `MappingStartEvent anchor=d 10..15 '&d\\n  '`, `AliasEvent d 46..48 '*d'`.\n- **Decision.** `yamlcore.compose` walks `YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True).parse(text)` and builds `RawScalar`/`RawMapping`/`RawSequence` itself (about 60 lines). It also builds:\n  - **`RawAlias(name, start, end, target)`** for each alias event. `target` is the anchored raw node most recently defined under that name, per the YAML rule that a redefined anchor applies to later aliases.\n  - **`RawAnchor(name, start, end)`** on the raw node of each event carrying `anchor`. Its span is the `&name` token.\n- **Refusals** (`LoadError`, exit 4, file and line in the message):\n  - any event with an explicit tag: `tags are not supported at line N` (C2);\n  - an alias whose name is not defined yet: `undefined alias *x at line N`;\n  - an alias to an anchor whose node is still open: `recursive alias *x at line N`;\n  - a second document: the same message as today for multiple documents.\n\n  ruamel's parse errors map exactly as today (`_describe`).\n- **Alternatives.** We could subclass ruamel's `Composer` to record alias marks. That ties us to ruamel internals across versions and still returns shared node objects. Positions recovered by scanning the text for `*` are fragile because of quoting and comments. Both rejected.\n- `values.parse_value` keeps refusing ancho...[truncated 14132 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Data Model: anchors and aliases stay honest\n\n## Raw nodes (`troupe/yamlcore.py`)\n\n| Type | Fields | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `RawAnchor` | `name`, `start`, `end` | Span of the `&name` token. |\n| `RawScalar` / `RawMapping` / `RawSequence` | + `anchor: RawAnchor \\| None = None` | `start` is the content start, never the `&` (R-2). |\n| `RawAlias` | `name`, `start`, `end`, `target: RawNode` | Span of `*name`; `target` is the most recent anchored raw node of that name. |\n\n`RawNode = RawScalar | RawMapping | RawSequence | RawAlias`.\n\n## Tree nodes (`troupe/tree.py`)\n\n| Type | Fields | Invariants |\n|---|---|---|\n| `Anchor` | `name`, `origin` (span of `&name`), `path: tuple[str, ...]` | `path` is where the anchored node is loaded in the merged configuration. |\n| `ScalarNode` / `MappingNode` / `SequenceNode` | + `anchor: Anchor \\| None = None` | An anchored node is one object, shared by every `AliasNode` referring to it. |\n| `AliasNode` | `origin`, `name`, `target: Node`, `anchored: Node \\| None`, `merge: bool`, `written: AliasNode \\| None` | Transparent to readers (`paths.unwrap`). Never a concrete container (placement does not look inside). A written alias has `merge=False`, `written=None`, `target is anchored`. A merge-derived alias has `merge=True`; it is never expanded. |\n| `MergeKeyMapping(MappingNode)` | + `written: MappingNode` | `entries` is the view: written entries, with the `<<` entry replaced in place by unshadowed merged-in entries (values wrapped as merge-derived `AliasNode`). Writers receive only `written`. |\n\n## Placements (`troupe/writeback/placement.py`)\n\n| Placement | Fields | When |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ExpandAlias` | `alias: AliasNode` (written), `parent: Collection`, `slot: str \\| int` | `--expand-alias` and the first alias on the route (or on the write side) is a written alias. |\n| (existing) `ReplaceScalar`, `ReplaceCollection`, `AddKeys`, `AppendItem`, `ConvertInclude`, `ReplaceText` | unchanged | Only for rou...[truncated 1272 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# CLI contract as delivered: Aliases (step 6)\n\nContract text (verbatim from the product owner, step 6):\n\n- Setting a path whose value is defined at an anchor (`&name`) changes it at the anchor, and so everywhere the alias is used.\n- Setting a path reached **through an alias** (`*name`) → exit 3; stderr mentions `--expand-alias`.\n- `set --expand-alias PATH VALUE` replaces that alias with a block-style copy of the anchored node, puts the end-of-line comment `# expanded from *<name> by troupe` on the key's line (two spaces before `#`), and then sets the value in the copy. Nothing else changes; other aliases still refer to the anchor.\n- Merge keys (`<<`) are never written.\n\n## Command surface\n\n```\ntroupe config set [--reason TEXT] [--in-include] [--expand-alias] PATH VALUE\n```\n\n| Exit | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Value set (or already equal: nothing written, nothing recorded). |\n| 2 | Usage error: including `--expand-alias` on `get`/`show`/`history`/`watch` (C10), and an anchor, alias or tag in VALUE (C14). |\n| 3 | Refused, no file changed: through an alias without `--expand-alias`; through `<<` (A-0); expansion impossible (C9); anchor not on key line on replacement (R-9). |\n| 4 | Configuration not loadable: tags (C2), anchors on includes or keys (C3), undefined/recursive alias, bad `<<` value, `<<` beside `$ref` (C13). |\n| 5 | Path errors, unchanged from step 1. |\n\n## Agent choices (spec A-0, C1–C14)\n\nSee [spec.md](../spec.md) \"Agent choices\" for each choice with its reason. In short:\n\n- A-0: `<<` is read with YAML merge semantics, and is never written through.\n- C1: reads are resolved.\n- C2: tags are refused.\n- C3: anchors and aliases may not touch includes or keys.\n- C4: a scalar alias counts as an alias.\n- C5: the anchor is kept on replacement.\n- C6: new keys follow the same rule.\n- C7: nested aliases are expanded outermost first.\n- C8: what goes in the copy.\n- C9: where the comment goes, and when expansion is refuse...[truncated 1099 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Quickstart: anchors, aliases and `--expand-alias`\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p /tmp/th/.troupe\ncat > /tmp/th/.troupe/troupe.yml <<'EOF'\ndefaults: &d\n  model: opus\ncoder:\n  settings: *d\nreviewer:\n  settings: *d\nEOF\ncd /work/repo\n\nuv run troupe config --theatre /tmp/th get coder.settings.model        # opus\nuv run troupe config --theatre /tmp/th set coder.settings.model sonnet  # exit 3, mentions --expand-alias\nuv run troupe config --theatre /tmp/th set defaults.model haiku         # everyone now haiku\nuv run troupe config --theatre /tmp/th set --expand-alias coder.settings.model sonnet\ncat /tmp/th/.troupe/troupe.yml\n# defaults: &d\n#   model: haiku\n# coder:\n#   settings:  # expanded from *d by troupe\n#     model: sonnet\n# reviewer:\n#   settings: *d\nuv run troupe config --theatre /tmp/th history\n```\n\nExpected history (after the import): one `set` event each for `defaults.model`, `coder.settings.model` and `reviewer.settings.model` (the anchor change). Then one `set` event for `coder.settings.model` (the expand-and-set).\n", "file_path": "/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8/quickstart.md"}

assistant: The plan is written and committed, so planning is done; no code has changed yet. The working tree is clean.\n\n**Branch**\n- We're on `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases`, which is also the planning base.\n- method reports `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases` as its merge target, and the current branch matches it.\n- Getting to `main` takes the local merge at the end, with no push and no PR.\n\n**The approach**\n- **Reading:** troupe will read the parser's events directly. The YAML library otherwise returns the shared anchored node for every alias, which loses where each `*d` is written. Anchored values keep the same positions as plain ones, so the existing writers don't change.\n- **Show and history:** a new alias node is passed through by readers, so `get`, `show`, `show --sources`, history and reconcile all see resolved values. Merge keys (`<<`) become a read-only view over the mapping as written.\n- **The refusal:** one function in `placement.py` decides "reached through an alias". It either refuses with exit 3, with `--expand-alias` in the message, or hands over to a new `writeback/aliases.py`. That module writes the block copy and the `  # expanded from *d by troupe` comment, then troupe reloads in memory and places the value again. The result is one verified write of one file.\n- **Safety net:** the "nothing else changed" check gains three rules. An alias still pointing at the same anchor counts as unchanged. On the route, and only with the flag, an alias may become a copy. Anchor names can't change outside PATH.\n- **Replacing an anchored value** keeps its anchor, so `set defaults '{...}'` keeps `&d`.\n\n**Spec changes you approved, each recorded with its reason:**\n- **C9:** `--expand-alias` exits 3 when the alias isn't on its key's or dash's line.\n- **C13:** `<<` beside `$ref` makes the file unloadable (exit 4).\n- **C14 / FR-010:** anchors and aliases in a `set` VALUE are refused (exit 2).\n\n**Main risks**\n- Every place that branches on tree-node types needs an alias case. Research R-3 lists all nine.\n- A file with deeply nested aliases expands exponentially in `show` and history. There are no performance targets, so this is noted rather than guarded.\n- The existing anchor and tag refusal tests from steps 1 and 5 will be updated to the new error messages; the exit code stays 4.\n\n**Commits**\n- `2a2999b` adds the plan.\n- `34643be` adds the other planning files and the spec changes, with the decision record.\n\nNext is `/method-command`. Bug `repo` stays claimed and open until the fix reaches `main`.\n\nFiles are in `spec/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8/`:\n- `plan.md`\n- `research.md`\n- `data-model.md`\n- `quickstart.md`\n- `contracts/cli.md`

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-a && python3 - <<'PYEOF'\np='src/troupe/yamlcore.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('\"\"\"The one place')\nend=s.index('import math')\ndoc='''\"\"\"The one place that knows the YAML library (ruamel.yaml) and the YAML 1.2 core schema.\n\nNo other module imports ``ruamel``: ``compose`` walks ruamel's *parser events* and builds the\nsmall typed ``Raw*`` dataclasses below itself, and ``resolve_plain`` resolves plain scalars\nourselves, because ruamel's own resolver is not the YAML 1.2 core schema (research R-13).\n\nruamel's composer is not used: it returns the anchored node object for an alias and so loses\nwhere the alias is written (research R-1). From the events, ``compose`` records every anchor as\na ``RawAnchor`` (the span of its ``&name`` token) on the node it is attached to, and every alias\nas a ``RawAlias`` (the span of its ``*name`` token) whose ``target`` is the raw node most\nrecently anchored under that name.\n\nAnchors and aliases are decided in one place, ``compose``'s ``allow_anchors`` switch. By default\nthey are refused with ``anchors and tags are not supported at line N``, exactly as before the\nswitch existed (``values.parse_value`` and the indentation probe rely on this). With\n``allow_anchors=True`` they are composed; an undefined alias, a recursive alias (one inside the\nnode its anchor is attached to) and any tag are refused (spec C2, FR-002). Refusals, like\nbefore, are raised only once the whole document parsed, so a parse error anywhere wins, and the\nfirst refusal in document order is reported.\n\nSpan behaviour (ruamel 0.19, verified by ``tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py``). ``start``/``end`` are\ncode-point indices into the composed string; a leading BOM is index 0 and CRLF counts as two.\nSpans are recorded exactly as ruamel's events report them (the same spans its composer gave);\nconsumers that need precise ends of block nodes must derive them from the text:\...[truncated 1796 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-a && python3 - <<'PYEOF'\np='tests/unit/test_values.py'\ns=open(p).read()\na=s.index('@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"text\", [\"*d\", \"&a 1\"')\nb=s.index('@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"text\", [\"{a: 1, a: 2}\"')\ns=s[:a]+'''@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"text\", \"problem\"),\n    [\n        # A lone alias has no anchor before it: unchanged from before anchors were composed.\n        (\"*d\", \"found undefined alias 'd' at line 1\"),\n        (\"&a 1\", \"anchors and tags are not supported at line 1\"),\n        (\"[&a 1, *a]\", \"anchors and tags are not supported at line 1\"),\n        (\"!t x\", \"anchors and tags are not supported at line 1\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_parse_value_rejects_anchor_alias_and_tag_with_usage_error(text: str, problem: str) -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(UsageError) as info:\n        parse_value(text)\n\n    assert info.value.exit_code == 2\n    assert info.value.message == f\"cannot parse VALUE {text!r}: {problem}\"\n\n\n'''+s[b:]\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nPYEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/unit/test_values.py tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py src/troupe/yamlcore.py", "description": "Make values test explicit"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-a; git show method/mission-troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8:src/troupe/yamlcore.py > /tmp/old_yamlcore.py; cat > /tmp/diff_fuzz.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, glob, importlib.util, itertools\nsys.path.insert(0, \"src\")\nspec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(\"old_yamlcore\", \"/tmp/old_yamlcore.py\")\nold = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(old)\nfrom troupe import yamlcore as new\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\ndef run(f, t):\n    try: return (\"ok\", repr(f(t, \"f.yaml\")))\n    except LoadError as e: return (\"err\", str(e))\n    except Exception as e: return (\"EXC\", type(e).__name__ + str(e))\ntexts = []\nfor p in glob.glob(\"tests/**/*.y*ml\", recursive=True) + glob.glob(\"**/*.yml\", recursive=True):\n    try: texts.append(open(p, encoding=\"utf-8\").read())\n    except Exception: pass\npieces = [\"\", \"---\\n\", \"# c\\n\", \"a: 1\\n\", \"a: &x 1\\n\", \"b: *x\\n\", \"c: !t x\\n\", \"d: !!str\\n\", \"e: &y\\n\", \"f: !t\\n\",\n \"g: [&z , *z]\\n\", \"h: {&w a: 1}\\n\", \"*q\\n\", \"- 1\\n\", \"i: &v\\n  j: 1\\n\", \"k: &u [*u]\\n\", \"l: [1,\\n\", \"...\\n\", \"---\\nm: 2\\n\",\n \"n: |\\n  x\\n\", \"&r o: 1\\n\", \"p: !!null\\n\", \"﻿\", \"q: 'a\\r\\n\", \"%YAML 1.2\\n---\\n\", \"r: &s\\n\", \"--- &t\\n\", \"--- !x\\n\", \"*nope\\n\"]\nfor n in (1, 2, 3):\n    for combo in itertools.product(pieces, repeat=n):\n        texts.append(\"\".join(combo))\ntexts += [\"\", \"---\", \"--- \\n...\\n\", \"!t\", \"&a\", \"*a\", \"&a [*a]\", \"!!str\", \"&a !t x\", \"[!t ]\", \"{? &k : v}\", \"a: &x # c\\nb: 1\\n\"]\nbad = 0\nfor t in texts:\n    a, b = run(old.compose, t), run(new.compose, t)\n    if a != b:\n        bad += 1\n        if bad <= 25: print(repr(t), \"\\n  OLD\", a, \"\\n  NEW\", b)\nprint(\"checked\", len(texts), \"diffs\", bad)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/diff_fuzz.py 2>&1 | tail -60"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-b && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/loader.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold_doc_end='''- overrides on a YAML target whose content is a list or scalar, not a mapping (D-7; an empty\n  target is an empty mapping and accepts overrides).\n\"\"\"'''\ns=s.replace(old_doc_end,'''- overrides on a YAML target whose content is a list or scalar, not a mapping (D-7; an empty\n  target is an empty mapping and accepts overrides).\n\nAnchors and aliases (spec C1, C2, C3; research R-3, R-5): files are composed with\n``allow_anchors=True``, so tags, undefined aliases and recursive aliases are refused by\n``yamlcore.compose`` (C2, FR-002). An anchored node is converted once, gets its ``Anchor`` and is\nrecorded in a registry keyed by the raw node's ``id``; an alias becomes an ``AliasNode`` whose\n``target`` (and ``anchored``) is that very node. Anchors precede their aliases in a document, so\nthe anchored node is always converted first. Anchors are per file: the registry is scoped to one\n``_load_yaml`` call, and an alias in an included file can only name an anchor of that file.\nC3 refusals, each naming the file and line:\n\n- an anchor on an include reference (an include string or a ``$ref`` mapping) or on a value\n  containing one at any depth: aliasing would make one include reachable from several paths;\n- an anchored mapping key, or an alias used as a key: keys must stay plain names.\n\nMerge keys (spec A-0, C13; research R-4): a key ``<<`` written plain (``'<<'`` is an ordinary\nkey) is a merge key. Its value must be an alias of a mapping, a mapping, or a list of those; the\nmapping holding it becomes a ``merge.merge_keys`` view (``MergeKeyMapping``) over the concrete\nmapping as written, ``<<`` entry included. A ``<<`` beside ``$ref`` in one mapping is refused\n(C13). tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The loader on files with anchors, aliases and merge keys (spec A-0, C1-C3, C13; R-3 to R-5).\n\nEach test writes real files under ``tmp_path`` and loads them with ``troupe.loader.load``; no CLI.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.merge import MERGE_KEY\nfrom troupe.paths import unwrap\nfrom troupe.tree import AliasNode, MappingNode, MergeKeyMapping, Node, ScalarNode, SequenceNode\n\nREPORTER = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\n\n\ndef _load(tmp_path: Path, text: str, files: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> MappingNode:\n    for rel, content in (files or {}).items():\n        target = tmp_path / rel\n        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        target.write_text(content, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    config = tmp_path / \"troupe.yml\"\n    config.write_text(text, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    root = load(config)\n    assert isinstance(root, MappingNode)\n    return root\n\n\ndef _at(node: Node, *keys: str) -> Node:\n    \"\"\"The value at ``keys`` as found (the last step is not unwrapped).\"\"\"\n    for key in keys:\n        mapping = unwrap(node)\n        assert isinstance(mapping, MappingNode)\n        entry = mapping.get(key)\n        assert entry is not None, key\n        node = entry.value\n    return node\n\n\ndef _scalar(node: Node, *keys: str) -> object:\n    value = unwrap(_at(node, *keys))\n    assert isinstance(value, ScalarNode)\n    return value.value\n\n\ndef _refused(tmp_path: Path, text: str, files: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> str:\n    with pytest.raises(LoadError) as refused:\n        _load(tmp_path, text, files)\n    message = refused.value.message\n    return message\n\n\n# --- Aliases share the anchored node (R-3) ---------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_alias_is_an_alias_node_sharing_the_anchored_node(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n ...[truncated 9503 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Reading configurations with anchors, aliases and merge keys through the real CLI (spec US1).\n\nSpec A-0, C1, C2, C3, C13; FR-001, FR-002. Reads (``get``, ``show``, ``show --sources``,\n``history``) see resolved values; unsupported anchor uses make every loading command exit 4,\nnaming the file. Writes through aliases are not yet supported: ``set`` is refused (exit 3) and\nnothing changes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, core, history_events, run_cli, snapshot\n\nREPORTER = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"get\", path)\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef test_get_through_alias_prints_anchored_value(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:  # US1-1\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": REPORTER})\n\n    assert _get(theatre, \"coder.settings.model\") == \"opus\\n\"\n    assert _get(theatre, \"reviewer.settings.model\") == \"opus\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_show_prints_resolved_values_without_anchor_alias_or_merge_syntax(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:  # US1-1, C1\n    text = REPORTER + \"base: &b {t: 1, list: &l [a, b]}\\nmerged: {<<: *b, own: *l}\\n\"\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": text})\n\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"show\")\n\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\")\n    assert result.out == (\n        \"defaults:\\n\"\n        \"  model: opus\\n\"\n        \"coder:\\n\"\n        \"  settings:\\n\"\n        \"    model: opus\\n\"\n        \"reviewer:\\n\"\n        \"  settings:\\n\"\n        \"    model: opus\\n\"\n        \"base:\\n\"\n        \"  t: 1\\n\"\n        \"  list:\\n\"\n        \"    - a\\n\"\n        \"    - b\\n\"\n    ...[truncated 4736 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-b && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/integration/test_set_overrides.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''# --- Agent choice (tracker crs, D-12 pending): an include with an anchor or tag is not converted ---\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"prop\", [\"&a \", \"!t \"], ids=[\"anchor\", \"tag\"])\ndef test_include_with_anchor_or_tag_is_not_converted(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, prop: str) -> None:\n    # Through the CLI the loader already rejects anchors and tags (exit 4, out of contract),\n    # so no file can change.\n    files: Files = {\"troupe.yml\": f\"coder: {prop}file:agents/coder.yaml\\\\n\", CODER: \"model: gpt\\\\n\"}\n    theatre = make_theatre(files)\n\n    result = _set(theatre, \"coder.temperature\", \"0.3\")\n\n    assert (result.code, result.out) == (4, \"\")\n    assert \"anchors and tags are not supported\" in result.err\n    assert snapshot(theatre) == _bytes(files)\n'''\nnew='''# --- Spec C2/C3: an include with an anchor or tag is not converted (the file does not load) ---\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"prop\", \"message\"),\n    [\n        (\"&a \", \"anchor &a at line 1 is on an include reference\"),\n        (\"!t \", \"tags are not supported at line 1\"),\n    ],\n    ids=[\"anchor\", \"tag\"],\n)\ndef test_include_with_anchor_or_tag_is_not_converted(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, prop: str, message: str\n) -> None:\n    # A digit-only missing segment below a\n  mapping would need either a mapping with a digit-only key (out of contract, C-004) or a new\n  list, which the contract does not describe, so it exits 5.\n\"\"\"''','''- Missing segments are only ever created as mapping keys. A digit-only missing segment below a\n  mapping would need either a mapping with a digit-only key (out of contract, C-004) or a new\n  list, which the contract does not describe, so it exits 5.\n\nAnchors, aliases and merge keys (step 6; spec FR-003, FR-004, FR-007, A-0, C4, C6; research\nR-4, R-6). \"Reached through an alias\" is decided here and nowhere else:\n\n- An anchored node reached directly (``defaults.model`` under ``defaults: &d``) is an ordinary\n  node: the rules above apply, and the write lands at the anchor (FR-003).\n- The route scan (``_route_aliases``) finds the first, i.e. outermost, ``AliasNode`` on the\n  concrete route to PATH's value (or, for a new key, to its deepest existing ancestor). It is\n  the first ``AliasNode`` among ``walk``'s nodes, plus aliases hidden as a side of a step-5\n  merged view when that side is the one the route actually takes. This covers PATH itself\n  being an alias (C4), an alias above PATH, a new key whose deepest existing ancestor is or lies\n  below an alias (C6) and values that come through a merge key ``<<``.\n- The write-side scan: a new key written on a side that is not on the route (the override or\n  base side of a merged view, or the ``route`` inside an include's holder mapping) is decided\n  the same way when that side is or passes an alias (``_on_side``, ``_on_base``).\n- One decision (``_through_alias``) for both scans: through ``<<`` is refused (exit 3) with or\n  without ``expand_alias`...[truncated 2660 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Expansion text: replace one written alias with a block-style copy (spec C7-C9; research R-7).\n\n``expand_edits`` returns the ``TextEdit`` values that turn a written ``*name`` into a copy of its\nanchored node, with ``  # expanded from *<name> by troupe`` at the end of the key's line (or the\nlist item's dash line). It sets no value: the caller (``apply``) applies the edits, reloads and\nwrites the value into the copy, one alias at a time, outermost first (C7).\n\nContract and choices:\n\n- C9 refusals (``WriteRefused``, exit 3, \"no file changed\"): the alias is inside a flow\n  collection; it is not written on its key's or dash's line; its line already has an end-of-line\n  comment; anything but blanks stands between the key's ``:`` (or the ``-``) and the alias.\n- C8 copy: rendered node by node from the anchored node's content, never by copying text ranges,\n  so comments are never copied and no ``&name`` is ever written (spans exclude anchors, R-2).\n  Keys and one-line plain/single/double scalars keep their written text; other scalars use the\n  step-1 rules (multi-line strings become literal blocks, ``blocks.render_new_multiline``).\n  Empty collections are ``{}``/``[]``. Flow collections are written in block style.\n- C7: aliases inside the copy stay aliases (``*name``). A mapping holding a merge key is copied\n  as written, so its ``<<`` entry is copied as is (its value stays an alias): it resolves to the\n  same values, and nothing is written *through* a merge key (A-0).\n- Placement (R-7): a mapping copy goes at the key's column + ``step``, a list copy at the key's\n  column + the parent mapping's sequence offset (flush if its nearest sibling list is flush, as\n  ``collections`` does). Under a dash, the copy goes at the dash's column + 2. Inside the copy,\n  nested mappings are indented by ``step``, nested lists by the same offset, and collection\n  items use compact form (``- k: v``), as ``collections`` renders new values.\n- A collecti...[truncated 14012 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/writeback/aliases.py'\ns=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()\nimport re\ns=re.sub(r'_BOM = \".*?\"\\n', '_BOM = \"\\\\\\\\ufeff\"\\n', s, count=1)\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n '_BOM =' src/troupe/writeback/aliases.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-c && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/writeback/placement.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ni=s.index('def place(')\nnew='''def place(\n    root: Node,\n    segments: list[str],\n    dotted: str,\n    *,\n    in_include: bool = False,\n    expand_alias: bool = False,\n) -> Placement:\n    \"\"\"Decide where ``set dotted VALUE`` is written.\n\n    Args:\n        root: The configuration as loaded from disk.\n        segments: The parsed, non-empty PATH.\n        dotted: PATH as the user wrote it, for messages.\n        in_include: ``set --in-include``: a new key goes into the included file (contract\n            *In-include*). It has no effect on existing values, list appends or refusals (D-9).\n        expand_alias: ``set --expand-alias``: a route through a written alias gives\n            ``ExpandAlias`` instead of a refusal (R-6). Merge keys are refused either way.\n\n    Raises:\n        WriteRefused: If PATH has a ``$ref`` segment (D-6), is reached through an alias without\n            ``expand_alias`` or through a merge key ``<<`` (R-6), names a mapping holding ``<<``\n            or would create a key ``<<`` (A-0), the whole value at PATH comes from a YAML\n            include, or PATH names a mapping both an include and an override define (D-10);\n            exit 3.\n        PathError: If PATH runs through a scalar, null or text include, uses a non-index segment\n            on a list, uses an index larger than the list's length, or needs a missing segment\n            that cannot be created (exit 5).\n    \"\"\"\n    if REF_KEY in segments:\n        raise WriteRefused(\n            f\"{dotted}: '{REF_KEY}' is not a setting; it names an include (no file changed)\"\n        )\n    found = walk(root, segments)\n    consumed = segments[: len(found.nodes) - 1]\n    through = _route_aliases(found, consumed)\n    if through is not None:\n        alias, depth = through\n        return _through_ali...[truncated 15905 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-c && uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py tests/unit/test_loader_aliases.py 2>&1 | tail -3; grep -rln \"place(\" tests | head"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Expansion text for ``set --expand-alias`` (spec US4, FR-005, FR-006, C7-C9; research R-7).\n\nEach test writes a real file under ``tmp_path``, loads it with ``troupe.loader.load``, finds the\nfirst written alias on PATH and its concrete parent, calls ``aliases.expand_edits`` and compares\nthe WHOLE new text. The reload check proves each copy is faithful (same resolved leaves).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.errors import WriteRefused\nfrom troupe.leaves import flatten\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.paths import parse_path, unwrap, walk\nfrom troupe.tree import AliasNode, MappingNode, Node, SourceFile\nfrom troupe.writeback import apply_edits\nfrom troupe.writeback.aliases import expand_edits\nfrom troupe.writeback.collections import detect_indent\nfrom troupe.writeback.placement import concrete_parent\n\nWORKED = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\nWORKED_EXPANDED = (\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  settings:  # expanded from *d by troupe\\n\"\n    \"    model: opus\\n\"\n    \"reviewer:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n)\n\n\ndef _load(tmp_path: Path, text: str) -> Node:\n    config = tmp_path / \"troupe.yml\"\n    config.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    return load(config)\n\n\ndef _expand(tmp_path: Path, text: str, path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Expand the first written alias on ``path`` and return the whole new text.\"\"\"\n    root = _load(tmp_path, text)\n    found = walk(root, parse_path(path))\n    alias = next(n for n in found.nodes if isinstance(n, AliasNode) and not n.merge)\n    located = concrete_parent(root, alias)\n    assert located is not None\n    parent, slot = located\n    file: SourceFile = alias.origin.file\n    assert file.text == text\n    edits = expand_edits(file, alias, parent, slot, detect_indent(text), path=path)\n    return apply_edits(text, edits)\n\n\ndef _r...[truncated 12109 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``placement.place`` through anchors, aliases and merge keys (spec US2, US3, FR-003, FR-004,\nFR-007, A-0, C4, C6, C7; research R-4, R-6).\n\nEach test writes a theatre, loads it with ``troupe.loader.load`` and calls ``place`` directly:\nuntil the applier handles ``ExpandAlias`` (WP05) the rule is checked at the placement level.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.errors import WriteRefused\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.paths import parse_path, unwrap\nfrom troupe.tree import (\n    AliasNode,\n    MappingNode,\n    MergedMapping,\n    MergeKeyMapping,\n    Node,\n    SequenceNode,\n)\nfrom troupe.writeback.placement import (\n    AddKeys,\n    ExpandAlias,\n    Placement,\n    ReplaceCollection,\n    ReplaceScalar,\n    concrete_parent,\n    place,\n)\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\nSHARED = (\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"  temperature: 0.2\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n    \"reviewer:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n)\nMERGED = (\n    \"base: &b\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"  temperature: 0.2\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  <<: *b\\n\"\n    \"  model: gpt\\n\"\n)\n\n\ndef _load(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, files: Files | str) -> Node:\n    if isinstance(files, str):\n        files = {\"troupe.yml\": files}\n    theatre = make_theatre(files)\n    return load(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n\n\ndef _place(\n    root: Node, path: str, *, in_include: bool = False, expand_alias: bool = False\n) -> Placement:\n    \"\"\"Place ``set path``; assert the placement holds concrete nodes only.\"\"\"\n    placement = place(\n        root, parse_path(path), path, in_include=in_include, expand_alias=expand_alias\n    )\n    for field in dataclasses.fields(placement):\n        value = getattr(placement, field.name)\n        assert not isinstance(value, MergedMapping | MergeKeyMapping), ...[truncated 12963 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('    before = flatten(_load(tmp_path, text))')\nend=s.index('def test_merge_key_inside')\nnew='''    old_root = _load(tmp_path, text)\n    before = flatten(old_root)\n    first = next(\n        i for i, n in enumerate(walk(old_root, parse_path(path)).nodes) if isinstance(n, AliasNode)\n    )\n    new = _expand(tmp_path, text, path)\n    root = _load(tmp_path, new)\n    assert flatten(root) == before\n    assert new.count(\"&\") == text.count(\"&\")\n    assert not isinstance(walk(root, parse_path(path)).nodes[first], AliasNode)\n    # Every old line but the alias's own is still there, in order (other aliases included).\n    old_lines = text.splitlines(keepends=True)\n    kept = iter(new.splitlines(keepends=True))\n    missing = [line for line in old_lines if line not in kept]\n    assert len(missing) == 1 and \"*\" in missing[0]\n\n\n'''\ns=s[:start]+new+s[end:]\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py 2>&1 | tail -60"}

Merge-key views resolve\nto the written mapping; replacing a '<<' mapping is refused.\" && git log -1 --oneline"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index('    first = next(\\n        i for i'):s.index('def test_merge_key_inside')]\nnew='''    nodes = walk(old_root, parse_path(path)).nodes\n    first, alias = next((i, n) for i, n in enumerate(nodes) if isinstance(n, AliasNode))\n    alias_line = text.count(\"\\\\n\", 0, alias.origin.start)\n    new = _expand(tmp_path, text, path)\n    root = _load(tmp_path, new)\n    assert flatten(root) == before\n    assert new.count(\"&\") == text.count(\"&\")\n    assert not isinstance(walk(root, parse_path(path)).nodes[first], AliasNode)\n    # Every old line but the alias's own is still there, in order (other aliases included).\n    old_lines = text.splitlines(keepends=True)\n    del old_lines[alias_line]\n    remaining = iter(new.splitlines(keepends=True))\n    assert all(line in remaining for line in old_lines)\n\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py 2>&1 | grep -B5 -A25 'def test_four' | tail -40"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''def _expand(tmp_path: Path, text: str, path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Expand the first written alias on ``path`` and return the whole new text.\"\"\"''','''def _expand(tmp_path: Path, text: str, path: str, step: int | None = None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Expand the first written alias on ``path`` and return the whole new text.\n\n    ``step`` defaults to ``detect_indent(text)``; pass it when the step must not come from there\n    (``detect_indent`` composes without anchors, so it falls back to 2 on anchored files).\n    \"\"\"''')\ns=s.replace(\"edits = expand_edits(file, alias, parent, slot, detect_indent(text), path=path)\",\n\"step = detect_indent(text) if step is None else step\\n    edits = expand_edits(file, alias, parent, slot, step, path=path)\")\ns=s.replace('''    assert _expand(tmp_path, text, \"coder.settings.model\") == text.replace(\n        \"    settings: *d\\\\n\",''','''    assert _expand(tmp_path, text, \"coder.settings.model\", step=4) == text.replace(\n        \"    settings: *d\\\\n\",''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py 2>&1 | tail -30"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d && (uv run pytest -q > /tmp/wp04-full.txt 2>&1 &) ; cat > /tmp/wp04probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport tempfile, pathlib\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.paths import walk, parse_path\nfrom troupe.tree import AliasNode\nfrom troupe.writeback import apply_edits\nfrom troupe.writeback.aliases import expand_edits\nfrom troupe.writeback.placement import concrete_parent\ndef ex(text, path):\n    d = pathlib.Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\"t.yml\").write_text(text)\n    root = load(d/\"t.yml\"); a = next(n for n in walk(root, parse_path(path)).nodes if isinstance(n, AliasNode))\n    p, s = concrete_parent(root, a)\n    try: print(repr(apply_edits(text, expand_edits(a.origin.file, a, p, s, 2, path=path))))\n    except Exception as e: print(type(e).__name__, e)\nex(\"d: &d\\n  m: 1\\nl:\\n  - settings: *d\\n    x: 2\\n\", \"l.0.settings.m\")\nex(\"d: &d\\n  t: |\\n    x\\nc:\\n  s: *d\\n      # deep\\n\", \"c.s.t\")\nex(\"d: &d\\n  m: 1\\nl:\\n- *d\\n\", \"l.0.m\")\nex(\"d: &d [1, {a: [x]}, []]\\nc:\\n  s: *d\\n\", \"c.s.0\")\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/wp04probe.py"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "WP04 follow-up decision: copying a `<<` entry int…", "message": "WP04 follow-up decision: copying a `<<` entry into the copy writes merge-key bytes, which the contract forbids (\"Merge keys (<<) are never written\", spec A-0/FR-007). Change expand_edits to REFUSE (WriteRefused, exit 3) when the node being copied contains a merge key anywhere (a MergeKeyMapping, or a written mapping with a `<<` entry, at any depth of the copy — but inner aliases are not descended since they stay aliases). Message format like your others: `{path}: the alias *{name} at {file} line {n} refers to a value that uses the merge key '<<'; copying it would write a merge key, which troupe never does; no file changed`. Replace the test that copies `<<` with a refusal test (whole text unchanged / exception message checked). Keep everything else. Run the unit tests for your module plus mypy/ruff (full suite not needed again unless you touch other files), remove __pycache__, commit (targeted staging, `feat(WP04): ...` with the Co-Authored-By line), and report the commit hash in under 10 lines. Work only in /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d.", "recipient": "a207477075f0c7201", "summary": "WP04: refuse expansion containing a merge key", "to": "a207477075f0c7201", "type": "message"}

A mapping holding a merge key is copied\n  as written, so its ``<<`` entry is copied as is (its value stays an alias): it resolves to the\n  same values, and nothing is written *through* a merge key (A-0).''','''- C7: aliases inside the copy stay aliases (``*name``).\n- A-0/FR-007: merge keys are never written, so a copy that would contain a ``<<`` entry (at any\n  depth, not looking inside inner aliases, which stay aliases) is refused (``WriteRefused``).''')\ns=s.replace('''    seq_offset = (\n        _sequence_offset''','''    content = unwrap(alias.target)\n    if _has_merge_key(content):\n        refuse(\n            \"refers to a value that uses the merge key '<<'; copying it would write a merge key,\"\n            \" which troupe never does\"\n        )\n    seq_offset = (\n        _sequence_offset''',1)\ns=s.replace('''def _concrete(node: Node)''','''def _has_merge_key(node: Node) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether copying ``node`` would write a ``<<`` entry; inner aliases are not descended.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(node, MergeKeyMapping):\n        return True\n    if isinstance(node, MappingNode):\n        return any(is_merge_entry(e) or _has_merge_key(e.value) for e in node.entries)\n    if isinstance(node, SequenceNode):\n        return any(_has_merge_key(item) for item in node.items)\n    return False\n\n\ndef _concrete(node: Node)''')\ns=s.replace(\"from troupe.paths import unwrap\",\"from troupe.merge import is_merge_entry\\nfrom troupe.paths import unwrap\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('    (\"base: &b\\\\n  x: 1\\\\nd: &d\\\\n  <<: *b\\\\n  y: 2\\\\ncoder:\\\\n  s: *d\\\\n\", \"coder.s.y\"),\\n','')\ni=s.index('def test_merge_key_inside_the_copy_is_copied_as_written')...[truncated 1590 chars]

assistant: `expand_edits` now refuses with exit 3 when the copy would contain a `<<` merge key, at any depth. Commit `2be21e6`.\n- **Rule:** it refuses if the copied node, or anything inside it, holds a `<<` entry. It does not look inside inner aliases, since those stay `*name`.\n- **Message:** exactly the format you gave.\n- **Tests:** the test that copied `<<` is gone, and its entry is out of the reload check. A new refusal test covers `<<` at the top of the copy and nested in a list item. It checks the full message and that the file is byte-for-byte unchanged. Another new test shows an inner `*m` pointing at a merge-key mapping is still copied as `*m`.\n- **Gates:** module tests 34 passed; mypy `Success: no issues found in 79 source files`; ruff check `All checks passed!`; ruff format `83 files already formatted`. I didn't rerun the full suite because only my two files changed.\n- `__pycache__` removed; working tree clean.\n- Still open from my first report: `detect_indent` falls back to step 2 on any file with anchors, which WP05 needs to fix.\n\nFiles: `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d/src/troupe/writeback/aliases.py`, `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-d/tests/unit/test_alias_expansion.py`

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e && uv run pytest -q -x tests/unit 2>&1 | tail -3 && git add src/troupe/writeback/apply.py src/troupe/cli.py src/troupe/commands/set_value.py && git commit -qm \"feat(WP05): set through aliases: expand loop, alias-aware verification, --expand-alias\n\nThe verifier now knows aliases (R-8): an alias stays the same alias of the same anchored node\nwithout descending, so a set at an anchor is legitimate everywhere it is seen; with\n--expand-alias the aliases on PATH may become copies differing only at PATH; anchors and\nmerge-key mappings are kept. apply_set expands outermost first, one file, verified once\nagainst the original tree; an unchanged value expands nothing.\" && git log -1 --oneline"}
tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/exp && cat > /tmp/exp/run.sh <<'EOF'\n#!/bin/bash\n# usage: run.sh 'yaml text' args...\nd=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $d/.troupe; printf '%s' \"$1\" > $d/.troupe/troupe.yml; shift\ncd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e\nuv run troupe config --theatre $d \"$@\"; echo \"exit $?\"; echo ---; cat $d/.troupe/troupe.yml; echo ---\nuv run troupe config --theatre $d history | grep '\"set\"' | cut -c1-90\nEOF\nchmod +x /tmp/exp/run.sh\nR=$'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n'\n/tmp/exp/run.sh \"$R\" set defaults '{model: x}'\n/tmp/exp/run.sh \"$R\" set defaults.temperature 0.2\n/tmp/exp/run.sh $'model: &m opus\\nx: *m\\n' set model sonnet\n/tmp/exp/run.sh $'defaults: &d {model: opus, t: 1}\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings.t 2\n/tmp/exp/run.sh $'m: &m opus\\ncoder:\\n  model: *m\\n' set --expand-alias coder.model x\n/tmp/exp/run.sh $'d: &d\\n  model: opus\\nagents:\\n  - *d\\n' set --expand-alias agents.0.model x"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set`` at an anchor and through an alias, through the real CLI (spec US2, US3).\n\nSpec FR-003, FR-004, FR-007, FR-009, A-0, C4, C5, C6, C11; NFR-001, NFR-003; research R-8, R-9.\nA set at the anchored node changes it for every alias and records one event per resolved leaf\nwhose value changed, in one batch with one lineage; a set reached through an alias or a merge\nkey exits 3, mentions ``--expand-alias`` and changes no file and no history.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, history_events, run_cli, snapshot\n\nCONFIG = \"troupe.yml\"\nREPORTER = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\nMERGED = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n\"\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"get\", path)\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _config(theatre: Path) -> str:\n    return (theatre / \".troupe\" / CONFIG).read_bytes().decode(\"utf-8\")\n\n\ndef _set_ok(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> list[dict[str, object]]:\n    \"\"\"Run a ``set`` that must succeed; return the events it recorded.\"\"\"\n    recorded = len(history_events(theatre))\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"set\", *args)\n    assert (result.code, result.out, result.err) == (0, \"\", \"\")\n    return history_events(theatre)[recorded:]\n\n\ndef _refused(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Run a ``set`` that must exit 3 changing nothing (files and history); return stderr.\"\"\"\n    recorded = history_events(theatre)\n    before = snapshot(theatre)\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"set\", *args)\n    assert (result.code, result.out) == (3, \"\")\n    assert result.err.startswith(\"troupe: \") and result.err.count(\"\\n\") == 1\n    assert snapshot(the...[truncated 6762 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/integration/test_set_aliases.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''    (\"value\", \"after\", \"read\"),\n    [\n        (\"{model: x}\", REPORTER.replace(\"opus\", \"x\"), \"x\\\\n\"),\n        (\"{model: x, t: 1}\", REPORTER.replace(\"  model: opus\\\\n\", \"  model: x\\\\n  t: 1\\\\n\"), \"x\\\\n\"),\n        (\"[a]\", REPORTER.replace(\"  model: opus\\\\n\", \"  - a\\\\n\"), None),\n        (\"5\", REPORTER.replace(\"&d\\\\n  model: opus\\\\n\", \"&d 5\\\\n\"), None),\n    ],''','''    (\"value\", \"after\", \"read\"),\n    [\n        (\"{model: x}\", REPORTER.replace(\"opus\", \"x\"), (\"coder.settings.model\", \"x\\\\n\")),\n        (\n            \"{model: x, t: 1}\",\n            REPORTER.replace(\"  model: opus\\\\n\", \"  model: x\\\\n  t: 1\\\\n\"),\n            (\"coder.settings.t\", \"1\\\\n\"),\n        ),\n        (\"[a]\", REPORTER.replace(\"  model: opus\\\\n\", \"  - a\\\\n\"), (\"coder.settings.0\", \"a\\\\n\")),\n        (\"5\", REPORTER.replace(\"&d\\\\n  model: opus\\\\n\", \"&d 5\\\\n\"), (\"reviewer.settings\", \"5\\\\n\")),\n    ],''')\ns=s.replace('''    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, value: str, after: str, read: str | None\n) -> None:  # US2-3, C5''','''    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, value: str, after: str, read: tuple[str, str]\n) -> None:  # US2-3, C5''')\ns=s.replace('''    assert _config(theatre) == after\n    if read is not None:\n        assert _get(theatre, \"coder.settings.model\") == read\n    else:\n        assert _get(theatre, \"reviewer.settings\") == _get(theatre, \"defaults\")\n''','''    assert _config(theatre) == after\n    path, out = read\n    assert _get(theatre, path) == out\n''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/integration/test_set_aliases.py 2>&1 | tail -2"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set --expand-alias`` through the real CLI (spec US4; contract worked example).\n\nSpec FR-005, FR-006, FR-008, FR-009, C4, C7, C8, C9, C10, C11; NFR-002, NFR-003; research R-8,\nR-10. Every alias on PATH is replaced by a block copy of its anchored node carrying\n``  # expanded from *<name> by troupe``, then VALUE is set in the copy: one write of one file,\nwhole files compared byte for byte. Refusals change no file and record nothing.\n\nThe last test calls the verifier directly (research R-8, plan Risks): an expansion that also\nreplaced another alias must be refused even though PATH reads back.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, history_events, run_cli, snapshot\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.writeback.apply import _same_outside\n\nCONFIG = \"troupe.yml\"\nREPORTER = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\nWORKED_EXAMPLE_AFTER = (\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  settings:  # expanded from *d by troupe\\n\"\n    \"    model: sonnet\\n\"\n    \"reviewer:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n)\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str, env: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre), env=env)\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"get\", path)\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _config(theatre: Path) -> str:\n    return (theatre / \".troupe\" / CONFIG).read_bytes().decode(\"utf-8\")\n\n\ndef _expand(\n    theatre: Path, path: str, value: str, *extra: str, env: dict[str, str] | None = None\n) -> list[dict[str, object]]:\n    \"\"\"Run ``set --expand-alias PATH VALUE`` that must succeed; return the events it recorded.\"\"\"\n    recorded = len(history_events(theatre))\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"set\", \"--expand...[truncated 11671 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/writeback/apply.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''Text includes follow research R-16 (revised)'''\nnew='''Anchors and aliases (step 6; spec FR-003-FR-005, C5, C7, C8, C11; research R-8):\n\n- ``placement.place`` decides whether PATH is reached through an alias; without\n  ``expand_alias`` that is a refusal (exit 3) before any rendering. A set at the anchored node\n  is an ordinary write, so every alias of it sees the new value.\n- Expand loop (``set --expand-alias``): while ``place`` returns ``ExpandAlias`` (outermost alias\n  first, C7), ``aliases.expand_edits`` replaces that alias with its copy in the current text,\n  and the configuration is re-loaded in memory from the texts so far (keyed by path: every load\n  makes new ``SourceFile`` objects). At most ``MAX_EXPANSIONS`` rounds. The ordinary placement\n  is then rendered against the expanded text; all edits land in one file (anchors are per file,\n  C-005), written once. An unchanged VALUE is checked first and expands nothing (no-op).\n- Verification runs once against the *original* tree, so expansion and set are verified (and\n  recorded, C11) together. ``_same_outside`` has three alias rules: (1) an alias stays the same\n  alias (name, merge flag, anchored node's path) and is not descended into, since its anchored\n  node is compared at its own place; (2) with ``expand_alias`` a written alias on the route may\n  become a copy, compared value by value with the anchored content and differing only at PATH;\n  (3) anchor names are kept everywhere outside a copy, including on the value at PATH (C5), and\n  a merge-key mapping stays one.\n\nText includes follow research R-16 (revised)'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new,1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff check src && uv run ruff format --check src | tail -1"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e\nT() { uv run troupe config --theatre \"$@\"; }\nmk() { rm -rf $1; mkdir -p $1/.troupe; printf \"$2\" > $1/.troupe/troupe.yml; }\nR='defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n'\nD=/tmp/rv5/a; mk $D \"$R\"; T $D history | wc -l; cp $D/.troupe/troupe.yml /tmp/rv5/a.before\necho \"== refusal\"; T $D set coder.settings.model sonnet; echo \"exit $?\"; cmp $D/.troupe/troupe.yml /tmp/rv5/a.before && echo same; T $D history | wc -l\nT $D set coder.settings.temperature 0.2; echo \"exit $?\"\nT $D set coder.settings '{model: x}'; echo \"exit $?\"\necho \"== expand\"; T $D set --expand-alias coder.settings.model sonnet; echo \"exit $?\"; cat -A $D/.troupe/troupe.yml; T $D history | tail -n +1 | cut -c1-200\nfor c in get show history watch; do T $D $c --expand-alias >/tmp/rv5/o 2>&1; echo \"$c exit $? stdout:$(T $D $c --expand-alias 2>/dev/null | wc -c)\"; done\necho \"== at anchor\"; D=/tmp/rv5/b; mk $D \"$R\"; T $D history >/dev/null; T $D set defaults.model sonnet; echo \"exit $?\"; cat $D/.troupe/troupe.yml; T $D history | cut -c1-160; T $D get reviewer.settings.model"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e\nT() { uv run troupe config --theatre \"$@\"; }\nmk() { rm -rf $1; mkdir -p $1/.troupe; printf \"$2\" > $1/.troupe/troupe.yml; T $1 history >/dev/null; }\nrun() { D=/tmp/rv5/$1; mk $D \"$2\"; shift 2; echo \"== $*\"; T $D \"$@\"; echo \"exit $?\"; cat -A $D/.troupe/troupe.yml; }\nrun m 'base: &b\\n  model: opus\\n  temp: 1\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *b\\n  temp: 2\\n' set base.model sonnet; T /tmp/rv5/m get coder.model; T /tmp/rv5/m history | grep '\"set\"' | cut -c1-90\nrun m2 'base: &b\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *b\\n' set coder.model x\nrun m3 'base: &b\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *b\\n' set --expand-alias coder.model x\nrun m4 'base: &b\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *b\\n' set coder.temperature 0.2\nrun u \"$(printf 'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n  name: c # hi\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n')\\n\" set coder.name bob\nrun u2 'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set newtop 1\nrun f4 'defaults: &d\\n    model: opus\\n    limits:\\n        max: 3\\ncoder:\\n    settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings.model x\nrun crlf 'defaults: &d\\r\\n  model: opus\\r\\ncoder:\\r\\n  settings: *d\\r\\nreviewer:\\r\\n  settings: *d\\r\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings.model sonnet\nrun crlf2 'defaults: &d\\r\\n  model: opus\\r\\ncoder:\\r\\n  settings: *d\\r\\n' set defaults.model sonnet"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-e\nT() { uv run troupe config --theatre \"$@\"; }\nmk() { rm -rf $1; mkdir -p $1/.troupe; printf \"$2\" > $1/.troupe/troupe.yml; T $1 history >/dev/null; }\nrun() { D=/tmp/rv5/$1; mk $D \"$2\"; shift 2; echo \"== $*\"; T $D \"$@\"; echo \"exit $?\"; cat -A $D/.troupe/troupe.yml; }\nrun s1 'm: &m opus      # keep col\\ncoder:\\n  model: *m\\n  other: 1        # user col\\n' set --expand-alias coder.model longervalue\nrun s2 'm: &m opus      # keep col\\ncoder:\\n  model: *m\\n' set m sonnet\nrun s3 'm: &m opus\\ncoder:\\n  model: *m  # mine\\n' set --expand-alias coder.model x\nrun c 'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings 5\nrun c2 'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings '{model: y}'\nrun bs 'm: &m opus\\ncoder:\\n  model: *m\\n' set --expand-alias coder.model 'a\nb'\nrun l 'd: &d\\n  model: opus\\nagents:\\n  - *d\\n  - *d\\n' set --expand-alias agents.0.model x\nrun n 'l: &l\\n  max: 1\\nd: &d\\n  limits: *l\\n  model: o\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings.limits.max 9\nrun fl 'd: &d {model: \"opus\", tags: [a, b], x: &x 1}\\ny: *x\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set --expand-alias coder.settings.model sonnet\nrun c5 'defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\n' set defaults '{model: x}'; T /tmp/rv5/c5 get coder.settings.model"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Acceptance for anchors and aliases: history, edge cases and refusals (WP06).\n\nEverything runs through the real CLI against real files and the real event store. Successful\nwrites compare the bytes of **every** theatre file; refusals check the exit code, an empty\nstdout, a one-line stderr naming PATH or the file, that no file changed and that no event was\nrecorded (NFR-001, NFR-003).\n\nCovered here: history, reconcile and ``watch`` of resolved leaves (C11, FR-009); the spec's\nEdge Cases (redefined anchors, anchors without aliases, aliases in an included file,\n``--expand-alias`` with ``--in-include``, undefined aliases, CRLF, ``show`` after expansion);\nand the refusals decided during implementation that had no real-CLI test yet (merge keys never\nwritten, recursive aliases, C14). The contract example, US2 to US4 and the read side are in\n``test_set_aliases.py``, ``test_set_expand_alias.py`` and ``test_aliases_read.py``.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import (\n    CliResult,\n    MakeTheatre,\n    WatchProcess,\n    core,\n    history_events,\n    recorded_within,\n    run_cli,\n    snapshot,\n)\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\ntype Event = dict[str, object]\n\nCONFIG = \"troupe.yml\"\nCODER = \"agents/coder.yaml\"\nREPORTER = \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  settings: *d\\nreviewer:\\n  settings: *d\\n\"\nCOMMENT = \"  # expanded from *d by troupe\"\nQUIET = 2.5  # seconds: longer than the 2 s watch contract, so \"nothing happened\" means nothing\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = _cli(theatre, \"get\", path)\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _bytes(files: Files) -> dict[str, bytes]:\n    return {\n        f\".troupe/{rel}\": content.encode(\"utf-8\") if isinstance(content, str...[truncated 4512 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8-lane-f && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/integration/test_aliases_acceptance.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s[:s.index('def test_watch_records_set_at_anchor_once_per_leaf')]\ns=s.replace('from pathlib import Path\\n','import time\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n')\ns=s.replace('''    _write(theatre, CONFIG, REPORTER.replace(\"  settings: *d\\\\nreviewer\", \"  settings:\\\\n    model: opus\\\\nreviewer\"))''','''    expanded = REPORTER.replace(\"  settings: *d\\\\nreviewer\", \"  settings:\\\\n    model: opus\\\\nreviewer\")\n    _write(theatre, CONFIG, expanded)''')\ns+='''def _seq_events(start: int, old: object, new: object) -> list[Event]:\n    \"\"\"The three ``set`` events of a change at ``defaults.model`` in REPORTER, from ``start``.\"\"\"\n    paths = (\"defaults.model\", \"coder.settings.model\", \"reviewer.settings.model\")\n    return [\n        {\"seq\": start + i, \"op\": \"set\", \"path\": path, \"old\": old, \"new\": new}\n        for i, path in enumerate(paths)\n    ]\n\n\ndef test_watch_records_set_at_anchor_once_per_leaf(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:\n    theatre = make_theatre({CONFIG: REPORTER})\n    with WatchProcess(\"--theatre\", str(theatre)) as watch:\n        watch.wait_ready()\n        before = history_events(theatre)\n        result = _cli(theatre, \"set\", \"defaults.model\", \"x\")\n        set_at = time.monotonic()\n        assert (result.code, result.out, result.err) == (0, \"\", \"\")\n        events = recorded_within(theatre, set_at, QUIET)  # longer than 2 s: no late duplicate\n        stopped = watch.stop()\n\n    assert len(before) == 3\n    assert core(events) == [*core(before), *_seq_events(4, \"opus\", \"x\")]\n    assert {e[\"via\"] for e in events[3:]} == {\"cli\"}\n    assert stopped == (0, \"\", \"\")\n\n\ndef test_watch_records_hand_edit_at_anchor_once_per_leaf(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:\n    theatre = make_theatre({CONFIG: REPORTER})\n    with ...[truncated 11510 chars]

(DM 01M48SQVABR08ZCXGCZD5S8GXV) |\",\n \"A `<<` value that is not a mapping, an alias to a mapping, or a list of those → exit 4. Because `<<` is never written, three more writes exit 3 with no file changed (found during implementation): adding a new key named `<<`; replacing as a whole a mapping that holds `<<`; and `--expand-alias` when the copy would contain a `<<` entry. (DM 01M48SQVABR08ZCXGCZD5S8GXV) |\")\nrep(S,\"writes the new value with `&d` kept, so every alias sees it. |\",\n \"writes the new value with `&d` kept, so every alias sees it. Moving an anchor off its own line would rewrite a line the user did not ask to change (TROUPE-001). |\")\nrep(S,\"If the alias is not written on its key's (or dash's) line (`settings:` with `*d` on the next line) → exit 3, no file changes. |\",\n \"If the alias is not written on its key's (or dash's) line (`settings:` with `*d` on the next line) → exit 3, no file changes. assistant: The fix is committed on `main` as merge commit `9a638fb`, and bug `repo` is closed. Nothing was pushed and no PR was opened.\n\nRunning the reporter's own file on `main`:\n- `set coder.settings.model sonnet` now exits 3, changes no file, and says why: `coder.settings is the alias *d … shared with its anchor &d; refusing to change it for every user (use --expand-alias to give this path its own copy); no file changed`.\n- `set --expand-alias coder.settings.model sonnet` produces the contract's example exactly (`settings:  # expanded from *d by troupe` with `model: sonnet` below it). `reviewer.settings.model` and `defaults.model` still read `opus`.\n- Setting `defaults.model` changes the value at the anchor, so every agent using it follows. History records one event per affected path.\n\n**Tests:** on the merged code, mypy and ruff are clean. The full suite failed once on `test_watch_never_changes_configuration_files` (2134 passed, 1 failed), then passed 2135/2135 on the rerun. That test passes on its own every time and this work didn't touch it, so it looks timing-sensitive; I filed a low-priority bug for it.\n\nThings you might not expect:\n- **Bug report vs. `main`:** the reported behaviour didn't reproduce. Files with anchors were refused outright at load (exit 4). So the fix adds anchor and alias support plus the contract's `set` rules, rather than changing existing write logic.\n- **Choices made where the contract is silent:** you approved them as my call, and each is in the spec with its reason. Main ones:\n  - merge keys (`<<`) are read the standard YAML way;\n  - any `set` that would write, rewrite or copy a `<<` exits 3;\n  - tags, and anchors on include references or keys, make the file unloadable (exit 4);\n  - expansion is refused (exit 3) when it would lose or move a user's comment or line.\n- **Behaviours found during implementation:** a few further exit-3 refusals and edge cases came up, for example a new key named `<<` and an expansion that would touch two files. I added them to the spec and the CLI contract before acceptance, so spec, code and tests agree.\n- **Acceptance:** it ran with `--lenient`, because the repo has no `docs/` directory; previous missions did the same.\n\n**Not done:** the optional post-merge mission review that method suggests.\n\n**Open question for you:** a plain `set` of a new key under an alias inside an included file is refused, even though step 5 would write it as an override that doesn't touch the shared value. That's the cautious reading of the "new keys under an alias" rule (C6). Allowing it would be a small change if you prefer.\n\nThe `fix/troupe-config-anchor-aliases` branch is still there. The spec, plan and acceptance records are in `spec/troupe-config-anchor-aliases-01M48SQ8/`.
